ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u1	Your Honor! I begin the 30th meeting of the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia. I salute you all very much! I am informed that they are detained and that today's meetings cannot be attended by the following Members: Richard Beumann, Aloise Peterle, Franci Rokavec and Tsveta Zalokar Orazem. We're moving to 21. Agenda item - First defence proposal of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Act on the Society for Automobiles in the Republic of Slovenia. The draft law was submitted by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia for consideration by the assembly. With regard to this law, a group of eleven Members, with first-signed Anton Anderlic, requested that the national assembly hold a general debate. I welcome the representative of the Ministry of Transport, Dr. Tomaž Vidica, State Secretary, and Prime Minister Dars, mag. Janez Christmas. I give the floor to the representative of the Government for further explanation of the bill. Please have five minutes to explain.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u2	Thank you so much for saying that. Dear Members and honourable Members! According to the current Dars Act, which was adopted almost ten years ago by the National Assembly, Dars is a public company in the form of a public company that organises and directs the construction of motorways, conducts financial engineering and manages and maintains Slovenian motorways. This law, together with the then gasoline tolar law, allowed Slovenia to start an intensive construction of the Slovenian highway network nearly ten years ago and thereby stimulate the economic development of the young country. Following the proposal of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Dars Act, Dars is to be transformed from a public company to a normal company, which will continue to be owned by the State, and the relationship between the State and Darsa will be governed by two contracts, as proposed by this Act. This would transform the mandate relationship so far into a contractual relationship, as is usually the case in the European Union countries, which is also one of the purposes of this proposed law. The proposed law also aims to facilitate the participation of private capital and other concessionaires in the construction of motorways, as Dars is the controller of the entire motorway network under the applicable law. The purpose of the law is also to rationalise business as much as possible or to reduce management and maintenance costs and to enable Darsa to become a more market-oriented economic society. As far as the area of construction is concerned, Dars will continue, on behalf of and on behalf of the state, i.e. in the so-called agent way, to organise and direct the construction of motorways in accordance with national planning documents and as so far will continue to carry out financial engineering. Dars will also continue to manage and maintain motorways, but not more than an agent of the state, but in his own name and on his own account, as an activity of an economic society. Following the adoption of this proposal, the State should therefore conclude two contracts with Dars: the so-called agency contract for the performance of the tasks relating to the organisation of the construction and renewal of motorways and the concession contract for the management and maintenance of motorways. The only founder and shareholder of the company will continue to be the Republic of Slovenia. This will attach a public highway maintenance company to Darsu by 31 December this year, thus eliminating the duality of organising one public undertaking to maintain motorways and collect tolls, that is Dars, and the other operationally, that is the regular maintenance and collection of tolls, operationally carried out, this is the Highway Maintenance Company. At the same time, under the proposed real estate law, which is currently in the management of both public enterprises, the state will increase the capital capital of the new Dars with real assets by raising the capital of the new Dars to about 11 billion Slovenian tolars. The proposed law strengthens the provision that the motorway body is public good, which makes it out of legal traffic. Dars will only be able to market the transit area that the state is transferring to the new Dars. The proposal for this law is in line with the proposal for a new national programme. The calculations carried out in the preparation of these two documents showed that during the whole period of the concession contract, the tolls would be sufficient to pay all the liabilities from the leased loans to complete the motorway programme. The liabilities of the credits hired for the construction of motorways will in no case be paid by the taxpayers, but by the users of the toll highway, and this was one of the main starting points in the preparation of this law. The proposed law will therefore regulate the organisation and management of the construction, management and maintenance of motorways as usual in the countries of the European Union. Allow me to conclude by stressing that the proposed law does not refer to any privatisation of Dars, on the contrary. The possible privatisation of Dars will only be decided on the basis of a preliminary decision of the National Assembly, up to a maximum of 49% of his value. Any privatisation of Dars during the intensive construction period would not be economically justified, it would be economically flawed and intensive construction would be carried out in line with the proposal for a new national road at least until 2008 at least. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u3	Thank you, Secretary of State Tomaž Vidic. The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. By the order of the applicants, the New Slovenia Group and Alojz Sok shall have the floor. Please.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u4	Dear Vice President, government representatives! Dear fellow Members and Members! In New Slovenia, we agree with the government's conclusion that it can no longer go as before. Budget and debt, loans in the long term can no longer provide sufficient resources to complete the national programme for the construction of Slovenian motorways. The government has now recalled the need to attract private funds and notes that this cannot be done through concession building, as other Member States of the European Union do. According to the data provided by the government itself, around 33% of the road programme in the European Union countries is built through concessions. In Novi Slovenija we are convinced that there was no effort in this direction, even though this provision is enshrined in the coalition contract, there was no attempt in this direction to try for any section of the motorway in the Republic of Slovenia to obtain the concessionaire, although this would be possible even at the present moment. We are convinced that at least the port of the highway is one that could be built with a concession. We could probably build the Maribor-Gruškovje road as well as some regional road in the Republic of Slovenia. The government is now legally changing Dars for the reason that the new company should continue to provide funding through bonds and the construction of the Slovenian road cross. This, of course, raises the question of why the government has not so far reached out to such a possibility and whether it is really necessary to change the status of the current organisation. The law that we have before us undoubtedly provides for the recapitalisation of Darsa, with private investors' money, which in fact means the sale of part of state property to interested private buyers. The government representative said that this would be within 49% of the total assets. A country that with tax money builds a dizzyingly expensive highway program is thus withdrawing from Darsa for which it will receive a purchase and a concession that is not specified in this law but is supposed to be a matter of mutual agreement. However, the price and concession are small compared to the value invested in the motorway programme. The purchase of the country will be incompatible with the contribution made by all Slovenian taxpayers. The Novi Slovenije Group is not the type of privatisation that the country has carried out in the case of Slovenian railways. It is not permissible to privatise the part of the highway that brings profits, but the part that is very expensive would remain owned by the Republic of Slovenia, i.e. by citizens and citizens. Privatisation is intended to provide a prerequisite for the inclusion of extra-budgetary funds in the financing of construction, maintenance and road management, and to enable the development and marketing of other activities from which the profit would be allocated to the construction and, in particular, the servicing of loans. The concessionaire will receive a great deal of value in management, as highways are undoubtedly and will not pay the corresponding economic compensation, but in return for the concession fee, the amount is not defined, it is marketed and it creates its profits, which should in fact be the property of the State and the taxpayers who invested in this system. Motorways are not some natural goods for which concessions are granted, as they need to be built first. The second question to be asked is whether privatisation of Dars will really increase its effectiveness. Dars is and will remain a monopoly, and the monopoly is more expensive and less quality, as he is certainly without real competition. The country is also a monopoly, but a public one. This means that control over its activity is public and the interest in winding up the prices of services is lower than that of the private owner. The proof of that is toll. Where the private sector has left the management of motorways, the tolls have not generally been abolished. However, State ownership or management of motorways from the experience of countries around the world has led to the abolition of tolls, which are in fact an economic burden, by dissuading the use of motorways and increasing operating costs. As far as tolls are concerned, it should also be noted that, at the moment, regional roads are not built in Slovenia and thus give users no opportunity to choose between road with toll or road, where toll is not payable. So this is a great manoeuvring of building our roads so far. It is known that the Company for the Motorways of the Republic of Slovenia also incorporates a number of telephone lines, cables that go to the expense of the taxpayer’s money. According to data from the end of July, Dars is expected to have approximately 230 km of optical cables taken along Slovenian highways. Although it uses this network for its own purposes, it will also market them in the future, as Dars has placed higher-capacity optical cables in construction than it needs for its own purposes. Privatisation will then be used by the state to market this investment by a private company that has not invested anything but will acquire water and generate huge profits. In Novi Slovenija we oppose such, I could say, a backlog of privatisation, where citizens will have only the costs and individual private companies with high profits. As we read this law, it seems that this is also the main point of this law, namely to create accompanying subsidiaries and then to carry out accompanying activities; one of these I have mentioned and today represents a trend of high technology and development, not only in the Republic of Slovenia, but throughout the world. The government wanted this law adopted by emergency procedure. Today we are talking about the law in the regular procedure, so we have gained some time. However, Members should receive answers to some of the questions raised when adopting this law. One such question is: Is there any need for a new company, or would it be better if the highway maintenance company simply joined Dars? The question is why the construction of highways in the Republic of Slovenia is so expensive. Every year, we build from 80 billion construction funds about 20 to 25 kilometers [20 to 25 km] of highways. It is clear that such means of the Slovenian road programme will not be completed in the next 10 years. However, when this programme is completed, new needs will, of course, arise. Even today, it is widely seen that in the Republic of Slovenia we should build tri-passes, not double-passengers, as we build them. We should have before us a thorough audit report that would answer why a kilometer of highway at such a price as quality. In fact, many comments have already been made in this National Assembly. Many are convinced that the quality of the construction of Slovenian motorways is poor and that the consequences of this poor construction quickly show up when the road is dedicated to its purpose. What should be answered about the contracts already concluded between Darsa and the various banks, the International Bank for Reconstruction and Development, which are the clauses in these treaties? Will the status change of Darsa also have any effect on the contracts concluded? The question should be answered as to how a new supervisory board of the new Dars will be formed. From the law before us, it seems that only representatives of the government will sit in this supervisory council, and not any more representatives of the National Assembly, and then, of course, the matter will be even easier in seclusion. How will the state control the market principles referred to in this law? Already there are many complications in the award of public contracts. I'm not going to mention individuals, you know there have been a lot of complications in public about this. What's the new toll system gonna do? Who's gonna set the tolls? At what height and on what they will depend, for this is one of the main sources on which this new company is to be based. In short, in New Slovenia, as I said at the outset, we are convinced that Dars needs to be reformed, but in a way that changes several articles of the law in the face of many uncertainties cannot be done. And such a way of changing the law, and consequently changing Darsa, has already essentially led to the possibility of corruption. We are in favour of economy, but we want this economy to be financially transparent, so that Members get sufficient reasoned financial explanations or reasoned reasons for this law, what we want to achieve with this law at all. On behalf of the New Slovenia Group, I propose that a public presentation of opinions be made on this law, because it is necessary to be aware that this is an extremely important law which will have major consequences for the further construction and management of large state assets. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u7	Thank you. Janko Weber, for the Group of Members of the United List of Social Democrats, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u8	Thank you for your word, chairman. A warm welcome to the representatives of the government and to fellow Members and fellow Members! In the UK Group of Social Democrats, we assess that the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Company for Motorways Act in the Republic of Slovenia is based primarily on the need to build a highway network in Slovenia and is based on several segments. In our view, these segments can be divided into costs for the implementation of the programme, the scope of the programme, into own resources for the implementation of the programme, debt and, of course, the cost-effectiveness of the implementation of the programme. If we look at the construction costs, we can find that the start of the motorway network was assessed relatively low, which is certainly due to the fact that when we took an intensive approach to the thinking and implementation of the highway network in Slovenia, we had relatively little technical documentation prepared and, above all, there were a lot of strangers when we put the route into space, which certainly led to the increase in the cost of project solutions and placements in space with the dynamics of the highway network. At the same time, the scope of construction grew. In fact, from the original scale, there was soon a very concrete desire to expand the highway network and the first rebalancing of the highway network can be seen that the programme was longer by 11% and that it was also more expensive by 112% than originally planned. It may have been questionable, above all, that, in the recent rebalancing of the highway network, new sections have come to the priority of those originally planned, which is likely to have contributed to this or unlikelyly, to the fact that costs have been increased on some sections or in the global construction of the highway network. So, from the very beginning, which was somehow in Slovenia in the construction of a highway network based on public funds, there is a rapidly growing need for these public funds to try to make as concretely as possible that the programme is not based only on budgetary resources, but that we are trying to create specific means to accelerate construction and, of course, was also a rather rational form of financing for the highway network. These two new sources, which have emerged at this time, are certainly a gas tolar, which we have just accepted because we have an additional quality resource, strictly designed to build a highway network. And of course, even this gas toll did not satisfy all the needs that were growing due to the volume of construction and due to the above-mentioned increasingly clear technical solutions. And the gas toll from the original planned had to be increased by about 25% at the first rebalancing. At the same time, the deadline for the collection of the gas toll has also been extended, which means that we have already been aware in the past, both in the government and in the National Assembly, of the need to follow, with our own resources and with this quality source of the highway network, otherwise we would have come up with great questions about when and how we will complete the motorway network. The gas toll is certainly the best source of the toll. However, the history of the toll system in Slovenia is extremely interesting, from the original closed system, which is certainly the most efficient system, even the most honest, we can probably claim, because you actually pay the actual toll. However, the history of the design and collection of the toll system has shown that we have fluctuated somehow from the most efficient system to the practical abolition of the toll system, which has led us to today have multiple forms of both closed and open system. And in the final phase, we are somehow trying to get closer to some optimal toll collection system that we have been trying to speed up and optimize, not least by adopting spatial laws that allow more efficient installation of toll stations on the already built highway network or on the future envisaged. Because it happens, however, that we all kind of want the highway network to get to our places as soon as possible. At the same time, however, we are faced with the problem of paying as little as possible for the fact that the highway network had been built, and this form was usually shown by trying to avoid paying the toll in one way or another. The road is one of those sources which, with some relatively economic construction of the motorway network and a relatively well-controlled and predictable form of liability for the construction of the motorway network, should be sufficient in particular to repay these leased loans. Today we can see that the toll itself certainly cannot service fully rented loans. For the scale of construction is significantly greater than this instant it is possible to provide funds through tolls. The debt, which is one of the forms of funding, has always been present in the construction of the highway network, both in domestic institutions and in international institutions. And, of course, this credit potential has reached that limit that we have planned in the existing laws, and above all, it has been linked to the fact that it does not really contain the capabilities shown by both the gas toll and the toll. And the analysis also shows that it will be necessary to use both the gas toll and the toll to pay off debts, i.e. two of the highest quality sources. And in order to avoid, however, the highway programme is stalled due to the service of the leased debts, it is of course necessary to transform the company for motorways, because the introduction and consideration of the possibility of financing the motorway programme also through private capital is probably one of the biggest reserves today that will allow us to accelerate the construction of the motorway network and which will certainly also contribute to greater efficiency and, of course, as I have already said, faster implementation of the programme. Therefore, we believe in the parliamentary group that the envisaged transformation of Dars under this law is essential if we are to accelerate the construction of the highway system, as we are moving, by public or public means alone, the deadline for the completion of the motorway system far into the future. We will certainly also face this problem when we consider the motion for a resolution on the national programme for the construction of the motorway network, because there is always a need for additional sections, after additional connecting sections, on additional fast roads and, lastly, after additional reconstruction of state-owned or parallel roads or those used at the time of construction, and then to be properly repaired and reinstated in the appropriate form for other state roads as well. And because one of the important objectives of the UK Group, or even a party to the United List of Social Democrats, is consistent with regional development, we believe that the efficient construction of the highway system can indeed lead to coherent regional development, and at the same time it is one of the most effective regional programmes that take place in the Republic of Slovenia. And as I have already said, we see the possibility of accelerating this development through both highway and regional resources, through additional resources provided by private capital through these forms, through the transformation of Darsa. Of course, the funds to be collected by issuing debt securities are a new form that has emerged and there will certainly be an additional argument here that all those who will or will be willing to invest their resources in this form of savings will also contribute to even greater control and to an even more cost-effective form of construction of the motorway network. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u11	Thank you. Jože Tanko has the floor for the position of the SDS Group of Members.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u12	Thank you, for your word. Dear colleagues! When I read the first sentence of the document by which the government accompanied the amendment of the Dars law into the legislative procedure, I thought very hard. How is that possible? Whether all preventive mechanisms and protectors have been cancelled, whether the financial and construction profession has failed, or whether it has failed policy, or whether, in the background, only the continuation of the transition transformation process and the ownership of the public property that is left is still there. Paying a gas tolar is a form of self-contribution if we admit it or not. And as we can see, a large part of this self-contribution, a lot of the means of this public gathering has gone up. I think that without the prejudices of many who have formulated national and road policy, we could ask for health, not only because of the unintended use of funds, but also because of what has been written in the explanatory memorandum by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. She wrote that all studies show that private capital must be attracted as soon as possible. But how many of these studies are there, and since when? It was probably the first to be produced ten years ago, and the amendment proposal has only been drafted now. It is obvious that our government has long been aware of the irrational approach taken in the construction of motorways, and it is also obvious that it has been making a decision and has waited for the extreme moment. After all, foreign experience also shows us that private capital has a great interest in the construction and management of transport networks and that it also performs these tasks quite well. However, the law provided for the possibility of private investment in the construction of motorways only a trick, yesterday the minister showed with an answer to Member Mamić's question. We are convinced that the proposed reorganisation approach is not the most appropriate, as it is obviously being set up and preparing a similar solution as for railways. What is not profitable will only become an even greater public burden, and what it bears, which brings profit, will be awarded by the State to the private and may be willing to charge a few percent of the concession revenue and leave the rest to the concessionaires. Instead of the full amount, the budget will gain only about 5% or 10%, maybe a percentage more. I also believe that it would be reasonable to tell Members clearly before deciding on the status of the new Dars, what the role of the National Assembly will be in the future, since the National Assembly no longer has the power to appoint members of the Supervisory Board. The number of members of the Supervisory Board shall be reduced from 11 to 6, 4 of which shall be proposed to the Ministry of Finance and Transport, and all 6 shall be appointed by the Assembly of the Society or Government. In short, there is no possibility of a member of the Supervisory Board becoming someone proposed by the opposition, which means that the company will operate completely without control. And that the crackdown on public finances will continue. It is also not clear from the explanation whether the new Dars will be obliged to operate under the Public Procurement Act and whether the Court of Auditors will be able to carry out an audit of the business of this company, given that it is converted from a public undertaking to a regular company after this proposal. And that the government of its minimum 51% can consist of various SODs and KADs as with the New Ljubljana Bank and thus avoid audit of the Court of Auditors. It also seems to us that one of the objectives of the reorganisation is to improve the image of the state of public debt, as Dars will no longer have the status of a public undertaking after the reorganisation and will no longer be part of the wider government debt. So one of the objectives of the reorganisation is also to fictitiously improve public finances. The explanation shows an extremely poor picture of the public sector as it is clearly stated in two or three places in the explanatory memorandum that Dars does not carry out his tasks in good and in full, that he does not manage the assets he manages. However, the objectives set out in the explanatory memorandum can be achieved and implemented without privatisation, since the objectives of companies do not differ by ownership, irrespective of whether the objectives are always similar in the public and private sectors. There is no financial justification for the law, no financial assessment of the effects of the changes envisaged. In short, the legislative process and the reorganisation of Darsa coalition brings the coalition back to its mind. And at least to us Members, such procedures are completely unrealistic and demeaning. In the opinion of our Group, the only good part of this proposal is to combine all activities linked to the highway programme. Let's go to the roadblocks, the control or the maintenance of the highways. We also know in the opposition that fragmented and divergent interests at the same facility are difficult to successfully monitor and effectively carry out the necessary activities. Therefore, it makes sense to join the highway maintenance company in order to enable business concentration and rationalisation of business. However, this can also be done in a simpler way. The status and reorganisation changes of such a large and important project as Dars are missing are a lot of things for a company that consumes almost 10% of the state budget. There is no analysis, no good explanation, no justification, and that is why our Group of Members or our Group of Members will not support this Act. Unfortunately, we cannot go to this project with our left hand, regardless of the current dominant political option. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u13	Thank you. Ivan Bozič, position of the SLS Group of Members, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u14	Thank you very much, Vice President. Madam Secretary, representatives of the government, Darsa, colleagues and colleagues! Good day to you all. It's a little sad today in our Parliament, as if we were talking before November 1st. The Slovenian People's Party's Group supports the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Dars Act and considers that the proposal for a document is appropriate for further consideration. This is based on the fact that the primary purpose of Darsa's status conversion from a public company to an economic company and the accession of public companies Darsa and the Company for the maintenance of motorways to a new Dars is to increase the cost-effectiveness of Dars' business in organising and managing the construction, management and maintenance of Slovenian motorways, while at the same time allowing private capital to enter the financing of the construction, management and maintenance of motorways and thus other concessionaries for sections that will not be built, managed and maintained by Dars. In other words, the area of organisation and management of the construction, management and maintenance of motorways will be demonopolized and market principles will be introduced and Dars will later act on the principle of profit and loss, with fundamental principles, thanks to which we intensively build motorways in Slovenia, although slower than we would like to, will also be strengthened. The organisation of the construction of motorways, the ability to borrow outside the budget, is a fundamental activity of Darsa, and this was also the fundamental purpose of the establishment of Darsa 10 years ago. The State has thus avoided the fact that credit obligations would become part of a public debt, which would probably prevent the Republic of Slovenia from meeting the Maastricht criteria. Then, too, Dars will lead, on behalf of the state, as an agent, the construction of those highway sections that will not be built by other concessionaires. The management and maintenance of the motorways already built and the collection of the toll is the second main task of the public company Dars today, with Slovenia being the only European country in which the management and maintenance of the motorway takes place in two public companies. The organisation of the management and maintenance of motorways and the collection of tolls in Dars, as a public undertaking, and the operational implementation of the maintenance of motorways and the collection of tolls under contract with Darsa, is operated by a public highway maintenance company. This duality is governed by the law as it is regulated in other European countries. Given that both public companies, Dars and the Motorway Maintenance Company are 100% owned by the State, their merger is only meaningful, as this allows for a more rational organisation of both companies. That is how we avoid duplication of certain general services. The accession shall also eliminate the negative parties arising from the current contractual relationship when each party is necessarily representing its interests. After the accession, interests will become common, with the addition of a highway maintenance company, an economically stronger company will be created, which will more successfully club foreign competition, which will be particularly important after our integration into the European Union. The successful market performance of the redesigned Darsa is also necessary for its recapitalisation, the main purpose of which is that that part of the state property, which is now a public asset in the management of the public undertakings of the Motorways Company of the Republic of Slovenia and the Motorway Maintenance Company of Slovenia, is to invest in the new Dars as a real asset of the State, and to enable investment in the development of activities through depreciation and additional revenues from these activities will reduce the need to refinance credit liabilities and, again, accelerate the repayment of loans from their own Dars resources. The equipment with which the state would recapitalise the new Dars in the form of a real-state input is mobile equipment for toll stations, mobile equipment for highway maintenance, facilities, in particular necessary for the maintenance and management of motorways, such as motorway maintenance bases. It is necessary to adopt the law as soon as possible, in the event that the status changes of public undertakings of the Association for Motorways of the Republic of Slovenia and the Company for the maintenance of motorways are not carried out before our full membership in the European Union, in order to ensure that the status quo is maintained. That would be a step back. Some positive aspects of its operation would also be lost; this is borrowing credit on its own, lack of financial barriers, relatively high autonomy and flexibility on the market. However, this solution, which means maintaining the current status or possible transformation into a legal public undertaking, would not be long-term. It is known that the European Union does not favour the existence of public undertakings because they distort competition in the common market, especially in areas where it is not necessary for services to be provided only by the State, but can be left to competition. In order to further develop and maintain and manage motorways within a public undertaking, it should be demonstrated that this is an area where the interests of the State are so important that it cannot be left to the market. In the European Union, where roads have been built and maintained on the basis of concessional relations for a long time in most countries, such a problem would be impossible to defend. Involvement of private entities in the exercise of these activities could be required by any entity on the market that would potentially be a provider and could not obtain concessions because the Republic of Slovenia would not have tendered. In this case, he could initiate proceedings before the European Commission, which would require the Republic of Slovenia to release competition in this area. The decision on the status changes of Dars should therefore take into account the following: If we want to change the management of motorways as a public economic service by content into a management of huge public goods, Dars has to become a public company. A shareholder may perform a public economic service on the basis of a concessional relationship. A statusal amendment to Darsa can be made by adopting a proposed law amending and supplementing the Dars Act. The reform is essential if before Slovenia enters the European Union, we want to ensure the management of Slovenian motorways in the public interest and in the interest of the Republic of Slovenia by maintaining the decisive influence of the Republic of Slovenia; that Dars, d.d., will be able to pay off all the burdens from the credits received, from the toll and other revenues of Dars and from the state budget; that credits will not be taken into account as the public debt of the Republic of Slovenia and that there will be no repayment of State guarantees in total of around EUR 2.4 billion from the capital; that capitalisation by the state of assets, which is now formally good and capital is dead, on the basis of the increased core capital of the service, it will also be possible to obtain capital from private resources, thereby allowing the Republic of Slovenia to recapitalise; that the capitalisation of the sector will continue to be 100 % and capital dead; that, primarily through the increased basic capital, through the service programme, also through the provision of the construction of private resources for the construction of private resources, and thus allowing the third-based financial resources; that it will be allowed to continue to continue to sustaining the capital. The proposed law shall take into account all those provisions. We are therefore convinced that it is necessary to adopt the proposed law as soon as possible, to join a public highway maintenance company and the Association for Motorways of the Republic of Slovenia in the new Dars and to recapitalise it with the largest possible real contribution of the country and to conclude the corresponding contracts with the new Dars before being a member of the European Union. On the basis of the above, it is essential that the whole process, including the conclusion of the Treaties, be carried out before the spring of next year, which is before the entry into the European Union. In view of this, the law should be adopted as soon as possible. I think that would mean a lot to the country. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u39	Nice hello to you all! Ladies and gentlemen of the House! Allow me to refresh your memory of the text and the background of this text of today's law on collective supplementary pension insurance. On 28 July this year, the government signed an annex to the collective agreement on non-agriculture with 11 unions, signatories to the collective agreement on non-agriculture, which is one of the basis for the creation of the law that we are considering today. It is therefore a partnership agreement created in the social dialogue, in which, through their union representatives, public sector employees have renounced the August escalation on the basis that their right to collective supplementary pension insurance will be regulated by law. A right that will arise in such a way that the law will provide for the payment of the premium of the collective additional pensionable account of denials to the August escalator. On the basis of this annex, the Government has drawn up a proposal for this law, which means some continuation of the reform of the pension system, which was justified by the Pension and Disability Insurance Act already in 2000, by the application of the possibility of the so-called second pillar of collective pension insurance. This is how the law regulates the supplementary collective insurance system at the origins of the other system law on pension and disability insurance. There are two, three specialities defined by the law. The first speciality concerns who may be the manager of a closed mutual pension fund. In addition to the management companies established by the system law, this law proposes, in agreement with the union, that the owners of the assets of the closed fund may also be insurance companies, banks, pension companies and the capital fund of pension and disability insurance. That's the first exception. The second, more important exception or speciality concerns tax reliefs. The amount of the premium paid under this Act, or after an annex, shall not be included, irrespective of the provisions of the Pension and Disability Insurance Act, as a basis for income or even as a basis for social security contributions of the insured. The amount of the premium shall also not be recognised to the employer as expenditure and tax relief in respect of the corporate tax. Well, there's a third specialty. Public servants, the so-called elderly, who are less than 10 years old before meeting the minimum conditions for obtaining the right to an old pension, shall have the opportunity to choose either a pension on that basis or to pay the ransom on the day of retirement. These are actually three basic features. Otherwise, the whole system is governed by the law on pension and disability insurance, as otherwise agreed in the annex signed with the government of trade unions. Under this law and annex, the lowest possible premium will be 4040 tolars and the highest 10,118 tolars. The premiums are already being charged, including for the month of August 2003, and everything must finally be prepared for the calculation and payment of the premiums in April 2004. It is therefore very important that the law be adopted by the end of this year. This is also our obligation from the annex, and we believe that it is an important improvement in the area of supplementary pension insurance in Slovenia, which will involve 140 000 public servants, and also a real agreement from the social partnership with the public sector unions. Thank you very much for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u50	Thank you. The views of the Youth Party will be presented by Marko Diaca.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u51	Thank you for your word. Nice greetings to all present! A proposal for a law on collective supplementary pension insurance for civil servants introduces a special scheme for supplementary collective pension insurance, such as that regulated by the system law, namely the law on pension and disability insurance. In a few points, the proposed legislation substantially changes the general regime, and the question is whether it puts us in a situation that is unconstitutional. The proposal introduces positive discrimination against public servants. This alone is due to the status of the public servant, which is the result of additional tax reliefs which are not granted to other insured persons under the general scheme. Such a distinction is only based on employment and is a violation of Article 14 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. Such arrangements will result in a reduced inflow of income from the pension and health allowance. The proposaler's claim that the proposed law has no additional financial implications is, therefore, only to the extent that there will be no additional outflows from the budget. However, the introduction of additional public service pension insurance under the proposed regime will constitute an outflow to the state budget and the budget of the pension and health institution. The proposed law also discriminates positively against public servants in an article where there is a specific arrangement for the payment of the redemption value. The general and applicable arrangements applicable to all other insurers provide that it is not possible to obtain additional pensions before the expiry of 10 years from the inclusion in the collective supplementary pension. This restriction is not available for public servants under the proposed law, or it is expressly possible to obtain additional pension rights even before 10 years of age. Discrimination will also take place among the employees themselves, as there will be significantly higher contributions for older employees than for young people. Member Kebrich also spoke about this in front of me. In the Young Slovenia Party, we also disagree with the inclusion of all public servants in a single mutual pension fund. This means that all public servants will be insured under the same conditions and also receive equal returns on their assets. This proposal is the most opportunistic loss for young people. For young people will be included in the mutual pension fund for as long as possible, so it would be appropriate for their funds to be invested to a greater extent in different forms of investment, of course, on the basis of their own decision, such as investment in shares, in smaller enterprises, in emerging capital markets and also in venture capital. This rule applies mainly because of the long-term nature of investments. As early as the first year of economic study, students learn that risky investments are more Vulative [[?]] , and in the long run, significantly more profitable than investments in low-risk instruments. Young people with conservative investment policies, which will apply uniformly to all civil servants, will have less resources in their pension account and thus lower additional pensions. With the creation of only one fund, all those involved for longer periods of time are deprived of additional pensions for higher pensions, but because of conservative investment policies they will have lower returns than they would have in more risky funds during the same period. However, management will depend on future additional pensions and thus the standard of living for future pensioners. The main objective of the law is to ensure a better material and social position of public servants after retirement. The contributions paid in the pension plan for supplementary pension insurance are, according to its content, part of the salary whose consumption is deferred to the future. By payment, a public servant acquires certain rights, on which the standard of living depends upon the level of the post-retirement standard. Protection and enforcement of these rights is the purpose of additional pension insurance. Investments in pension plans must be safe and effective. Therefore, the separation of the manager from the sponsor is an essential step in ensuring the security of investments. Any link between the operator and the institution providing additional pension premiums should be legally prohibited. As a rule, it is an employer, in the case of public servants it is the Republic of Slovenia. In fact, it is only in this way that it is possible to ensure the full independence of the operator, which is a prerequisite for effective management by the means of future pensioners. In the Party of Youth of Slovenia, we propose that the proposer already enters into the law the establishment of several mutual pension funds which will differ in the risks of investment policy. Each individual public servant should decide which of the established mutual funds will be included, on the basis of information, of course. Public servants do not have a choice between salary increases and supplementary pension insurance, but they must at least have the option of selecting a mutual fund on which the amount of their pension will depend in the future. In order to avoid doubts as to the constitutionality of the bill, it is also necessary to allow other insurance undertakings to be regulated in the manner proposed for public servants. Additional tax advantages should also be extended to insurers under existing general arrangements. The Government should bring the bill into substance in line with the directive on activities and supervision of institutions implementing pension plans for supplementary pension insurance. Member States of the European Union must harmonise national legislation by 25 September 2005 at the latest. The aim of the Directives is to simplify the rules for the operation of pension plans in the Member States, to allow the free movement of both employees and pension plan managers between Member States and to ensure a high level of safety and efficiency in the operation of supplementary pension schemes. The Directive introduces high mandatory information standards for members involved in pension plans as each member is obliged to submit annual balance sheets and to report the annual High Requirements report are also introduced with regard to the mandatory introduction of criteria for the management of investment policy and standards for investment, auditing and actuarial evaluation. Members of the Young Slovenia Party cannot support the current solutions to the law. If the government takes our proposal into account when preparing the law for second reading, we will reconsider the support of the law.

PERIOD OF THE DAY - This is, first of all, a proposal for a law on the permanent residence of foreigners with the nationality of other successor countries, formerly in the Republic of Slovenia, which had permanent residence in the Republic on 23.12.1990 and 25.2.1992 in the Republic of Slovenia. The draft law was submitted by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia for consideration by the assembly. With regard to this law, a group of eleven Members, with first-signed Anton Anderlic, requested that the national assembly hold a general debate. First, I will invite the Minister of the Interior to further explain the bill and then you will get the floor from the representatives of the groups of Members. First, the Minister of the Interior. Please.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u68	Respectable. The Government, as the proposer of legislation in the field of foreigners, is firmly committed to finalising this issue in a legally correct manner, with two proposed laws. With this moment under consideration, and with the so-called law on the enforcement of the 8th point of the constitutional decision. The adoption of these two laws will eliminate individuals who have suffered from a loss of status. On the other hand, we will enforce, establish a condition for the enforcement of a constitutional decision and thereby strengthen and enforce the rule of law. The bill is selective and contains assurances that those who have acted against the interests of the Republic of Slovenia in an unlawful way and have also threatened the highest values recognised by the civilized world will not be able to enforce their status under the terms of this Act, of course if they are found guilty of crimes before the judicial authorities, including the worst crimes against humanity and international law, as well as other crimes defined by Slovenian criminal law. The main solutions to this law are to establish legal bases for obtaining status for all those who have not yet taken advantage of these options, as well as for their young children. The law also lays down the conditions and procedure for determining decisions on permanent residence for those who have obtained a permanent residence permit on the basis of relief and for those who have obtained a permit or who have subsequently acquired a nationality. In all these cases, the law provides for the effect of the decision issued in return; what is expressly provided for in the Constitutional Court decision. It is essential that we follow the Constitutional Court's decision in order to lay down criteria for determining the legitimate absence from the country. We follow the one-year rule as justified absences and lay down some exceptions to the permissible annual absence. I would like to focus your attention on the so-called negative criteria. Indeed, negative criteria set out situations where a foreigner has left the Republic of Slovenia and has not notified the competent authority thereof and such abandonment is considered to be a violation of permanent residence and a negative criterion for obtaining the right to status. These criteria are fully stated in the law, and criteria are also laid down or the criteria set out in the law on foreigners are applied. It is an expulsion of a foreigner in a way that clearly shows the permanent abandonment of Slovenia. It is a matter of emigration to another country, to a state of nationality, for the purpose of permanent association with family members. Negative criteria include those linked to the actual cessation of life in the Republic of Slovenia due to the security measure imposed, expulsion from the country if it is an individual who is in the records of a competent authority as an international offender, if he comes to the country in order to be connected with the exercise of illicit activities. However, it does not meet the condition of actual life, nor does the alien who has committed crimes expressly defined by the law. This means that there are reasons to suspect that it may pose a threat to public order and peace, to security, to the international relations of Slovenia, if there is any suspicion that its stay in the country will be linked to the conduct of violent acts and so on. In short, the law lays down a number of negative criteria that provide essentially a selective approach, which is necessary in this regard, when it follows the constitutional court's instructions in a faithful manner when the condition of permanent residence is determined. I would like to conclude this brief introduction by commenting on or explaining the financial consequences of this law. I repeat that the law itself does not produce direct financial consequences, except for those related to the issue of decisions or to organisational logistics issues. The Ministry of the Interior has so far adopted 7 claims for compensation, which courts have not yet decided on. Any speculation, speculation as to the extent to which these damages, what the state will suffer, do not yet have serious professional grounds. The adoption of this law will no longer result in compensation claims, as the legal basis for their submission is already in the Constitutional Court’s decision, and therefore the proposal for such settlement is also pointless and without appropriate professional grounds. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u70	Thank you so much for saying that, Mr. President. Respectable. The story of the so-called deleted, the story of the people who were deleted from the register of the permanent population of Slovenia in February 1992 and entered into the list of foreigners, began on the basis of a law on foreigners adopted in the summer of 1991, which provided in its 81st Article that, pending the finalisation of the decision in the administrative procedure for admission to the citizenship of the Republic of Slovenia, the provisions of this law do not apply to nationals of the former Yugoslav republics who, within 6 months of the application of the law on citizenship, applied for citizenship of the Republic of Slovenia under the 40th Article of this Act. For SFRY nationals who are nationals of another republic who have not applied for Slovenian citizenship within the six months or who have been issued a negative decision, it has been stipulated that they will be subject to the provisions of the Aliens Act two months after the expiry of the period within which they could apply for citizenship or from the issue of a final decision. After the then applicable law, foreigners could stay in Slovenia without special permission for up to three months or for a period granted in a visa and only on the basis of permission. Foreigner may first have been granted temporary residence permit in Slovenia. After at least three years of continuous residence in Slovenia, he was granted a residence permit. It subsequently extended the deadline for obtaining a permanent residence permit from three to eight years. This period also includes the adoption of a new law on foreigners in 1999, which provides much more detail for the rules of the game as regards licences. At the same time, a new law on foreigners was adopted on the basis of a decision of the Constitutional Court to regulate the status of citizens of other countries as successors of the former SFRJ in Slovenia, which fell by April this year by a decision of the Constitutional Court. This law allowed foreigners who lived permanently in Slovenia on 23.12.1990 and 25.6.2001 respectively, and who had been living continuously in Slovenia since then, to obtain a permanent residence permit in compliance with the law of certain conditions. In order to submit applications, the law laid down three months and at the same time could apply for permanent residence permits for those who applied for citizenship, and a negative decision had been issued to them under Article 40 of the Citizenship Act. Therefore, the Constitutional Court annulled the law in April this year because it did not recognise the status of permanent residence as abrogated from the date of expiry, because it did not arrange for the granting of a permanent residence permit to forcibly removed aliens and because it had set a short time limit for the lodging of applications for permanent residence permits. With new solutions it is expected to cover the status of around 4,000 people, but there are no precise data on how many of them actually live in Slovenia and how many of them want to regulate their status in Slovenia. This is a very important and sensitive political issue that the ruling coalition wants to resolve with two laws. And if we can, to some extent, support the proposal for a law that regulates the issue of decisions to revert, i.e. the enforcement of the 8th point of the Constitutional Court decision, we have so much greater concern about the proposal for a law that we are discussing and dealing with the regulation of the situation of those who have not obtained any status so far. The beneficiaries were repeatedly called upon to regulate their status in accordance with the law, but not all did. Since 1992, it has regulated 7,310 citizens of the former SFRJ, 4,210 under both foreign laws, and 3,100 under the law on the regulation of the status of citizens of other countries, the successors of the former SFRJ in Slovenia. According to the experts of the Council of Europe, our regulation regarding citizens of other Yugoslav republics with independent legislation in 1991, when all those who had permanent residence were able to acquire the citizenship of the Republic of Slovenia if they decided to do so, was in line with all European standards. By October 1995, the United Nations Committee on Legal Affairs had also found compliance of Slovenian legislation with the standards of the United Nations. In its decision, the Constitutional Court drew attention to three rights in which they could have been revoked, namely the right to advance a military pension, the right to a protective allowance and the inability to replace a driver's license, which were subject to constitutional examinations. However, the Constitutional Court in its decision did not provide guidance for the resolution of the situation backwards or for the correction of possible injustices by regional laws. The Ministry of Labour, Family and Social Affairs, as well as the Ministry of the Interior, are expected to have considerable work to do to issue decisions 7310 to individuals on official duty. The work is definitely going to be huge. In fact, according to the proposal of the law, we should turn our lives around for all those potential beneficiaries and identify different life situations. In doing so, we ask ourselves whether retroactively resolving the status of so-called deleted is the right thing to do. Proveting the condition of real life for a adult alien or for a minor child is often extremely difficult, but many have also been abused. What about the assessment of the financial implications for the country? The proposaler does not want to discuss this and as a future issue. In the Slovenian People's Party's Group, we were surprised and concerned to adopt a decision by the Constitutional Court on the law governing the status of citizens of other countries, the successors of the former Socialist Federal Republic of Yugoslavia, in particular as regards the decision in paragraph 8 of the said Constitutional Decision adopted in April this year. It is necessary to take account of the fact that point 8 of the decision is about legal innovation. Our Constitution stipulates that the legal consequences of the decisions of the Constitutional Court are governed by law and also dictates the principle of legality under the General Administrative Procedure Act. We therefore support the implementation of the two laws, the specific law for the implementation of this 8th point and the general law for all other cases. As soon as the constitutional decision was adopted in the SLS Group at that time, we decided to support the implementation of the constitutional decision, but only with the relevant law. In the Slovenian People's Party, we believe that it is always necessary to respect the decisions of the Constitutional Court and that the legislator must prescribe a means of implementation. We would expect to take equal account of certain deadlines for the harmonisation of laws with those of the Constitutional Court, in particular those relating to the general public or to the rights and problems of Slovenian citizens, such as the Constitutional Decision on the Income Act, on the facilitation and maintenance of children, or on the constitutionality of the Koper municipality and so on. We also expect that, with regard to the implementation of the other points of the Constitutional Decision, the law before us will truly take account of all the actual situations and situations in which the citizens of other countries are the successors of the former SFRJ, as laid down in the Constitutional Decision. At the same time, it leaves it to the legislator to define the criteria and conditions under which the State will allow or grant a permanent residence permit. Our country has already defined, by means of acts of independence and later by means of a special law on the regulation of status, the less favourable conditions for regulating the status of citizens of other countries, heirs to the Socialist, Federal Republic of Yugoslavia. In the Slovenian People's Party's Group, we believe that the draft law follows a constitutional decision or even exceeds it in regulating the status of those who have already acquired citizenship and in the provisions relating to family members or beneficiaries who have died. We must take into account the unequivocal facts in the drafting and adoption of the law, which confirm that many who do not have a regulated status do not wish to do so or intend to do so for various reasons, including on grounds of opposition to the independence and establishment of our country. In our view, such aliens should not be granted status or even facilitated by the conditions for their acquisition. Whoever at the time felt belonging and solidarity with Slovenian citizens was able to arrange the documents under the then legislation, so they do not hold back the claim that the deadlines were too short or that the conditions were too harsh. At the time of creation, the country was obliged and entitled to verify who will become a citizen and who is or will be able to reside as a foreigner on the ground of Slovenia. This is also evidenced by the fact that the competent authorities sent approximately 7 thousand invitations to those who did not regulate their status, of which some 2 000 were actually served, while the others returned with a note "moved" or "unknown".In 2000, between 500 and 700 people responded to the invitation of the administrative authority, either in accordance with the provisions of the Aliens Act or the Law on Nationality of the Republic of Slovenia. The State must not simply re-assign the status because it turns out that some people did not actually stay here, but they could have acquired citizenship because at that time the state did not require or verify the conditions of actual residence. Unfortunately, however, the law before us does not cover all the actual situations and situations in which they have been, or have been, applicants or beneficiaries of a residence permit. The law which has been incorporated into the parliamentary procedure does not contain the last part of the provision that protects and foresees: "or other values which are protected by the criminal law of the Republic of Slovenia in accordance with the provisions of the first paragraph of Article 4 of the Constitutional Law for the implementation of the basic constitutional instrument on the independence and independence of the Republic of Slovenia..." the end of the quote. Secondly, such an act aligns the values acquired by the independence and victims of the war on Slovenia. Thirdly, the provision referred to an act for which a foreigner was convicted, so such a State cannot be trusted. Fourthly, in the Aliens Act, there is a reason for denying suspicion, so it is not a more acute but a more lenient provision, as provided for in the Constitutional Decision. So it's just a proven and not just a suspicious act. We therefore believe that the law should also contain such a provision. We are aware that there are various reasons for them. We can conclude that many are also concerned with speculative, in particular financial interests towards our country. ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u193	Your Honor! I welcome the representative of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia! We're moving to 23. PERIOD OF THE DAY - THE FIRST DEFINITIVE PROPOSAL OF THE STRANGE AND HUNTING LAW. The draft law was tabled by a group of Members with first-signed Anton Anderlic. With regard to this law, a group of 12 Members with the first-signed France Čebul requested that the national assembly hold a general debate. First, I give the floor to the representative of the proposers to the draft law to further explain the bill. Mr. Anderlich, please!
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u194	Your Honor! I regret that no Minister of Agriculture has in the last eleven, twelve years, nor has any government been able to propose a law on savagery and hunting. It was therefore almost imperative that we approach this law in a different way. This is a group of Members following a previous broad debate and also correspondence with the Ministry of Agriculture. So after 1991, several laws were passed that significantly affect the area of game and hunting. The Environmental Protection Act provides that wild game is state property, the Forest Act is an intervention in the field of hunting and breeding planning, where the Institute for Forests of the Republic of Slovenia is designated as a carrier of planning. It further introduces new forms of plans, but does not abolish the previous ones, which stem from the law in force in 1976, namely the law on savagery and hunting. When we dealt with this law, we examined everything that is now in force in the country of the Republic of Slovenia and what is happening in other countries around us, especially in the countries of the European Union. The law takes into account all European directives in this field, as well as the international conventions signed and ratified by the Republic of Slovenia. It should be pointed out that the rule of law on game and hunting is not part of the European acquis or the uniform European legislation. Therefore, it was neither the subject of the accession negotiations of the Republic of Slovenia with the European Union nor the subject of the coordination of ours with the European acquis. The European Union represents the principle of subsidiarity in the field of game and hunting, which means that it does not unify legislation in this field, but leaves it to the Member States to regulate the area of savagery and hunting in the same way as best suited to their historical and traditional traditions and to their natural and cultural characteristics. The Member States of the European Union therefore have very different hunting laws and systems. In doing so, even within one country, for example France and Italy, there are several hunting systems. The second principle of the law focuses on the conservation, protection and sustainable use of wild game species by hunting and on improving their habitats. So, in ensuring the ecological, social and economic functions of the game and its living space. The basic solutions to the bill are: the game is state property; the hunting right belongs to the Republic of Slovenia, which also allocates it; the minimum hunting area is 2 000 hectares; the introduction of concessions for all fisheries awarded by the Republic of Slovenia; the Republic of Slovenia may maintain or establish fishing sites with special purpose, in particular with a view to maintaining and maintaining the natural habitats of the game, in particular endangered species in the characteristic environments of Slovenia. Let me draw attention to some of the key innovations brought by the draft law under consideration. The draft law introduces concessions to be paid by operators of hunting grounds as concessionaires to the State. The preferred beneficiaries of the concession are local communities, through which owners of agricultural and forestry land will also be paid in the form of indirect or direct benefits. The bill specifies the criteria for the creation, assessment and payment of damage to game. While the situation in this area is not critical, harmful cases are dealt with in agreement between the operator and the injured party. According to some assurances and data, only 1% of the harmful cases ended up in court. There are some problems with cross-border game, especially pigs in Prekmurje. That is why we believe that this model is suitable for the future. The bill clearly distinguishes two pillars of hunting, on the one hand unprofessional hunting, and on the other hand professional hunting or hunting for a particular purpose. The proposal is implemented by the Slovenian Institute of Forests as a public institution, which, through professional and transparent planning of wild activities, represents the interests of the country as owner of wildlings and the general public. I'm sorry. If I can combine that second part of 5 minutes, which is also reported at this same point, I would now take another 2 minutes, end up saving 3 minutes, because as a proponent I have twice reported after 5 minutes. No, no! Continue and subtract from that final time if you can. Time is reported. It's only divided into two parts. It could be 10 minutes once.

ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u198	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. The government has examined the draft law and adopted the opinion I am giving you below. This proposal was adopted by the government without a vote against. In such a way that this government opinion was inter-sectorally coordinated. I would like to say that, above all, from a professional point of view of nature conservation, the proposed arrangement is a good solution in the Government's view, as it regulates the definition of spatial units for the planning and execution of wild game in many surface-environmental areas, taking into account the ecological conditions and life needs of game. It is positive because the planning of wildlife operations at different levels, based on the biological and ecosystem requirements of individual species and characteristics of the ecosystem, is regulated in a comprehensive manner. It is positive because it stipulates that plans for hunting areas are produced by a public professional institution and provides for the close cooperation of nature-security services in the preparation and adoption of plans. The bill is positive because it fully regulates the achievement of the ecological function of the game and the care for a balanced relationship between the wild and other plant and animal species and man, and uses, as a starting point, that the game is under the direct care of the State. However, the Government has quite a few comments on this bill. First of all, it is important to clarify the concept of wild play and to bring it into line with the applicable legislation on the use of natural goods. It is important to note that the concept of game should be defined in such a way that it meets the requirements of European Union law. It is also important to note that individual plans for sustainable wildlife management should be included in forest management plans. We estimate that the proposed solution, which is inappropriate for sustainable wildlife management, is the tasks of concessionaires as a public service. The government opposes that. We also have a number of other comments. However, these are comments on the basis of which the Government notes that the proposed legal text provides a conceptual solution to the area of game and hunting. Individual solutions, which need to be legally and professionally verified and finalized, will be drafted by the Government in an amendment form and enforced in the further process of adopting the Wildlife and Hunting Act. Thank you very much.

Furthermore, the establishment of hunting grounds with a special purpose should be solely and solely with a view to maintaining and maintaining the natural habitats of game, in particular endangered species. Farmers and forest owners should also participate in the organisation of hunting activities. Where there is an interest in the organisation of hunting activities by farmers and forest owners, they must also be professionally qualified to do so, or they must ensure that they carry out their professional hunting activities or are adequately trained. When organising hunting activities in an area, farmers and forest owners meeting professional standards must take precedence over other organisations having an interest in the pursuit of hunting activities in the same area. Thus, game management must be in line with the comprehensive planning of professional institutions, taking into account the knowledge and experience of farmers and foresters, who are both hunters and best knowers and natural system maintenanceers as a whole. Therefore, in the Party of Young Slovenia, we expect the proposer to take our comments today into account when preparing the law for second reading. Today, as I mentioned, we will support him at first reading.
