ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-28-SDZ3-Redna-30.ana.u216	Thank you very much. Madam Vice-President, distinguished representatives of the government, colleague and colleague! In the Parliamentary Group of the Liberal Democracy of Slovenia, we also see this law on hunting and game as a law that touches on the area of relation to civilizational acquisitions. That is why this law is very important. In fact, it touches on the civilisational gains that citizens and citizens of Slovenia have and are used to. It is also at first sight perhaps a simple matter, such as freedom to walk into the woods, freedom in limited quantities to gather mushrooms. For we are not used to being caught among some of the inscriptions "prohibited passage", "private property", "the violators will be punished". This law therefore deals with important blocks covering the relation to the ecological function of forests and game, as well as to the social function of forests and hunting. A humane, sonar attitude toward animals has been incorporated into the marriage. The law also provides a basis for a reasonable economic function of hunting with its meaning, both domestic and foreign tourism. And the law also involves a responsible attitude towards one of the largest and most important social organisations in our country, i.e. the hunting union of which about 22,000 members has done much in the past to make high-level hunting in Slovenia. We believe, of course, that there is an in place, a realistic approach to respect for the results, to achieve what has been achieved, including a critical assessment of those things that are to be improved in the future. The law also provides, of course, among other important categories, for the payment of compensation, for the burden of concession, as a very important constitutional element of this law. In the presentation of the Group's position, I would like to be brief and focus on one of the most important issues, this is the issue of ownership, because individual thought finance is very important here. Our view is that modern countries have abandoned some category of absolute property inviolability. We need to make that clear. Even the Slovenian state, especially if we look at paragraphs 33 of the Constitution and 67 of the Constitution, is one of the countries which has such a modern view of this category. In fact, Article 67 of the Constitution clearly defines the functional principle of ownership, its content and its considerations. The way property is acquired and consumed therefore ensures its economic, social and environmental function. Natural resources and natural resources include wild game animals, and Article 70 of the Constitution provides that the law lays down the conditions under which these natural resources may be exploited. Thus, the implementation of constitutional principles has already been made, this is the 1993 Environmental Protection Act, which provides in Article 17 that natural resources, such as water, mineral raw materials, wild game animals and fish, are the property of the Republic, are state property. In our view, the constitutional principles adopted by this law also apply to the writing and complementing of the new hunting law. The acquisition and use of property on land and forests should therefore not jeopardise the ecological functions of the forest. If we have a bit of a problem, of course we know the position of the beneficiary forest owners' association, but we are convinced that full compliance with these principles would have led to a cycle of time of 154 years ago. Indeed, almost nothing has changed since the Imperial patent of the Austrian monarchy. Therefore, we will insist, taking into account all the useful proposals, on the principle that social states do not practice an absolute category of property which has also recognised to the land owner, for example, property on minerals and everything on the ground and beneath it. With regard to the European view on these issues, the following. The ownership concept of the Slovenian Constitution in the accession process to the European Union has never been disputed. You could say it the other way around. Article 259 of the Treaty establishing the European Community specifically provides that this European Association Treaty shall in no way affect the property law of the Member States. Accordingly, it was not the subject of negotiations either, so the Slovenian decision on the ownership of wild animals was not the subject of negotiations. Of course, we agree with the proposals that the interests and the role of landowners must be taken into account to the maximum extent possible, while, of course, we must be aware that forests are today a fairly solid habitat. Today the property is owned by about 450,000 owners. Of these 450,000 are only 123 according to our data of those who own over 100 hectares of forest, that is about 03 mills of owners. It is also important that the analysis shows that in Slovenia, without a change of constitution, no law on hunting can be drawn up based on the absolute ownership of land. In this regard, it seems important to us to find that 40% of the hunters are farmers. It is said that the issue under consideration has been quite widely examined and, therefore, according to our information in the law, the proposed solutions enjoy quite broad support and are in the public interest. As regards the coordination of interests, the use of space and the protection of nature in relation to hunting, I would say the following. In Slovenia today, it is quite successful in the context of the annual hunting communication plans, which may not have been here to the extent that everyone wants it together. However, public discussions on the proposed plans are taking place within the framework of the Forestry Office and each of the landowners has the possibility of communicating with civil society organisations now and, of course, we will try to strengthen this possibility by participating in the discussions and making comments. Therefore, while we are aware of all the minorities or important issues that still need to be finalized in this law, while preserving the basic solutions of this law in the parliamentary group, we support this proposal. Thank you.
